# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_54372182.pdf
 
 
1 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE DEFINING THE TERMS AND CONDITIONS OF 
EMPLOYMENT FOR SWORN OFFICERS OF THE CHEYENNE POLICE 
DEPARTMENT FOR THE FISCAL YEAR 2023-2024." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  Definitions. 
 
 
EMPLOYEE/EMPLOYEES - Employee or employees as used in this ordinance means 
sworn police officers, below the rank of chief. 
 
 
Section 2.  Applicability. 
 
 
The terms and conditions of employment as set forth herein shall apply to all employees as 
defined herein. 
 
 
Section 3.  Purpose. 
 
 
The purpose of this ordinance is to define the terms and conditions of employment for 
employees.  The rights contained herein are in addition to any rights an employee may have under 
the Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual (as that document may be amended at any time, 
before or after passage of this ordinance), and applicable federal, state and local laws.  In areas of 
conflict between this ordinance and established federal, state or local laws, the latter shall govern.  
This ordinance comprises the exclusive statement of employee terms and conditions of 
employment.  Any other agreement with or representation made to any employee by a supervisor, 
agent or officer of the City of Cheyenne which is in conflict with or in addition to this ordinance 
is specifically disclaimed by the City of Cheyenne, except for the Cheyenne Police Department 
Policy Manual and except for agreements entered into by and between supervisory personnel and 
employees establishing specific job expectations, requirements or discipline which are part of the 
employee’s official personnel file maintained by the Human Resources Department. 
 
 
Section 4.  Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual and Benefits Policy. 
 
 
The Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual including the benefit policy set forth in 
Exhibit A, as they exist or are amended from time to time, are incorporated herein and made part 
of these terms and conditions of employment and shall apply only to sworn police officers. 
 
 
Section 5.  Compensation. 
 
 
The compensation paid to employees is set forth in Exhibit B, which is attached and 
incorporated by reference herein.  The compensation appropriate for the aforementioned 
employees shall be computed with reference to the grade of the individual employee.  Assignment 
of employees to the appropriate grade under the new plan will be made on a case by case basis, 
5:26 pm, Jul 03 2023

 
 
2 
based upon employee hire date or promotion date, by the chief with the assistance of the director 
of Human Resources.  The governing body reserves the right, in its sole discretion, to modify or 
change pay scales.  Effective July 1, 2017, employees will receive, in addition to basic pay, 
specialty on call pay as set forth in Exhibit C, attached hereto and incorporated by reference herein.  
Effective January 1, 2022, employees will receive, in addition to basic pay, longevity pay as set 
forth in Exhibit D, attached hereto and incorporated by reference herein. 
 
Effective July 1, 2022, for any employee called to active military duty, the City of 
Cheyenne will compensate the difference between the employee’s City base pay and total military 
monetary compensation (commonly listed on the military leave and earnings statement as the total 
entitlements), provided the military compensation is lower than the City base pay.  Base pay is 
defined as the regular pay the employee would have received from the City had they not been 
called to active military duty not including on call or specialty pay.   
 
 
Section 6.  Continuation of Employment. 
 
 
Any employee covered by this ordinance who continues his or her term of employment 
during and beyond the term of this ordinance shall be entitled to advancement annually (subject to 
the provisions of Section 5), as set forth in Exhibit B, upon recommendation of the employee’s 
supervisor, if the mayor and council approve funds to pay the increases. 
 
 
Section 7.  Health, Medical, Dental, and Life. 
 
 
The City will negotiate with or seek bids from qualified health insurance providers and will 
enter into a contract with a qualified carrier or carriers to provide employees with a medical 
insurance plan, which is on file in the Human Resources Department, for fiscal year 2023-2024.  
Each uniformed City employee eligible for health insurance coverage shall contribute ten percent 
(10%) of the monthly health insurance premium attributable to the level of coverage selected by 
that employee.  This payment will be a payroll deduction. 
 
 
The City will maintain dental insurance on employees pursuant to the dental plan on file in 
the Human Resources Department.  The City will pay premiums for employees.  If an employee 
wishes to maintain dental insurance under this plan for eligible dependents as defined in Appendix 
B of the dental plan, he or she may do so by paying the premium for the additional person(s) 
insured. 
 
 
The City will pay premiums for term life insurance on all employees in accordance with 
the term life insurance plan on file in the Human Resources Department and City Clerk’s Office. 
 
 
Section 8.  Term. 
 
 
The terms and conditions of employment set forth in this ordinance shall apply for fiscal 
year 2023 – 2024 and shall be approved as part of the budget process.  This ordinance will 
terminate upon passage of a replacement ordinance. 
 
 
Section 9.  Repeal of Other Ordinances. 

 
 
3 
 
 
All other ordinances establishing specific terms and conditions of employment are 
repealed. 
 
 
Section 10.  Severability. 
 
 
If any clause, sentence, paragraph or part of this ordinance or application to any person or 
circumstance shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction to be invalid 
such judgment shall not affect, impair or invalidate the remainder of this agreement. 
 
 
Section 11.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its passage and 
publication. 
 
 
FIRST READING:  
 
____________________________________ 
 
SECOND READING: 
 
____________________________________ 
 
THIRD AND FINAL READING: ____________________________________ 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR 
 
 
(SEAL) 
 
ATTEST: 
 
_____________________________________ 
KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK 
 
 
Published:_____________________ 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 


 
 
1 
EXHIBIT A 
 
EMPLOYEE WAGE AND BENEFIT POLICY 
 
THIS POLICY COVERS ALL COMMISSIONED OFFICERS OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
POLICE DEPARTMENT. 
 
1. 
MONTHLY WAGES. 
 
 
a. 
Wages. 
 
 
Effective July 1, 1997, all commissioned officers became covered by the City of Cheyenne 
pay plan. 
 
 
All step increases shall be on the officer’s original date of employment or promotion date.   
 
 
b. 
Lateral Transfer Program. 
 
 
Lateral entry candidates must be currently certified as a Wyoming peace officer or in a 
state with a reciprocity agreement with Wyoming.  If not currently certified in Wyoming, lateral 
entry candidates must take and pass the Wyoming POST challenge test, as well as the Wyoming 
criminal law and domestic violence courses offered through the Wyoming Law Enforcement 
Academy. 
 
 
Subject to these requirements, the department offers a lateral transfer program for officers 
with prior qualifying law enforcement experience.  All officers will be hired at a Grade 201 - Step 
1.  Officers completing a minimum of six months of the department’s eighteen-month probationary 
period may, at the chief’s discretion, be placed in the step program according to their experience 
as follows: 
 
 
 
2 to 4 years of experience: 
Grade 201 - Step 3 
 
 
4 to 6 years of experience: 
Grade 201 - Step 4 
 
 
6 + years of experience: 
Grade 201 - Step 5 
 
 
c. 
Specialty On Call Pay. 
 
 
Effective July 1, 2004, eligible employees will be entitled to receive specialty on call pay.  
Employees are limited to receiving compensation for a maximum of two specialties per month. 
 
 
An eligible employee is one who serves as a first line supervisor (field training officer) or 
is required to be available for callout and does not receive any other compensation for being on 
call. 
 
 
d. 
Longevity Pay. 
 

 
 
2 
 
Effective January 1, 2022, eligible employees will be entitled to receive longevity pay 
based on years of service.  Longevity raises will be effective on the employee’s anniversary month. 
 
2. 
OVERTIME. 
 
a. 
Patrol Officers and Patrol Sergeants. 
 
 
All sergeants and officers who are called in for overtime duties will receive a minimum of 
two and one-half hours of overtime pay, excepting court appearances which are limited to two 
hours of overtime pay or compensatory time if the time spent is two hours or less.  All court time 
in excess of two hours is to be paid at time and one-half the classification rate.  All patrol officers 
and patrol sergeants shall be limited to eighty hours of accumulated compensatory time, at which 
time, they must take paid overtime until they reduce below the eighty-hour level. 
 
 
All officers assigned acting sergeant duties will be compensated two hours overtime for 
each shift.  All sergeants assigned acting lieutenant duties will be compensated one-hour straight 
time for each shift. 
 
 
Should the acting sergeant, lieutenant or captain duties extend beyond thirty consecutive 
days, the department may temporarily promote the officer, and place the officer in the appropriate 
pay grade of the new position. 
 
3. 
HOLIDAYS. 
 
 
The officers and sergeants of the City of Cheyenne Police Department will be entitled to 
those paid holidays that are set by Personnel Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne, as 
amended from time to time, and as enjoyed by personnel of other departments of the City, to 
include “Special Holidays” or other paid time off afforded to other City employees. 
 
 
The rate of pay for these holidays will be computed on the basis of 2,080 hours per year.  
The officer working a holiday has the option of taking overtime pay or compensatory time at one 
and one-half times the classification rate.  If the holiday falls on an officer’s regular day off, he 
will be given eight hours of straight pay or compensatory time.  Likewise, if the holiday falls on 
the officer’s vacation, or while on sick leave, the holiday will not be counted as vacation time or 
sick leave. 
 
4. 
SICK LEAVE. 
 
 
Each officer shall accumulate sick leave time at the rate of ten hours for each month of 
continuous employment to a maximum of six hundred forty hours. 
 
 
Officers who have completed four years of continuous service and terminate from the City 
shall receive 50% of their accrued sick leave at their regular base rate, not to exceed 240 hours.  In 
the case of retirement, an employee will be reimbursed fully for his or her accrued sick time up to 
240 hours. 
 

 
 
3 
 
Officers who have not accumulated 480 hours at the beginning of the calendar year will be 
entitled to twenty-four hours of goodie time each year, provided they use not more than forty hours 
of sick leave during each calendar year.  Officers who accumulate 480 hours at the beginning of 
the calendar year will be entitled to forty hours goodie time each year, provided they use no more 
than twenty-four hours of sick leave each calendar year; or thirty-two hours goodie time each year, 
provided they use no more than thirty-two hours of sick leave each calendar year; or twenty-four 
hours goodie time each year, provided they use no more than forty hours of sick leave each 
calendar year.  Goodie days under this section shall not be accumulated.  Officers entitled to the 
goodie time provided herein shall make application to the Chief of Police who shall schedule the 
goodie time having regard for the convenience of the department.  Upon termination, the twenty-
four, thirty-two or forty hours of goodie time are not paid as vacation. 
 
 
For a period of one year following the date of an injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-
102(a)(xi) sustained in the line of duty for the Cheyenne Police Department, an officer who is 
totally disabled from performing any duties for the Department shall receive a monthly payment 
from the City in an amount equal to the difference between: (1) the amount of any monthly benefit 
received by the officer from any division of the Wyoming Department of Employment; and (2) 
the average amount of monthly pay received by the officer during the twelve month period 
preceding the date of injury, provided however, that if the officer has less then twelve months of 
service prior to the injury, his or her average monthly pay shall be computed on the basis of actual 
months of service.  Upon the expiration of a period of one year following the date of an injury as 
defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi), an officer who is totally disabled from performing any duties 
for the Cheyenne Police Department shall be required to use accumulated sick leave.  
 
5. 
SICK BANK. 
 
 
The Sick Bank is to be used by officers in the case of serious illness or injury which is not 
covered under Worker’s Compensation, and after they have exhausted their accumulated sick 
leave. 
 
 
The Sick Bank is to be established on the effective date of this policy with a balance of 
1,200 hours contributed by the City.  The Sick Bank will maintain a balance limited to 1,200 hours.  
This balance to be renewed by the City, if any days are used, on January 1st of each year. 
 
 
A contributory pool (Sick Pool) of 4,160 maximum sick hours will be established on the 
effective date of this policy.  Sworn officers with excess sick leave (over 640 hours) may contribute 
their excess sick leave to the Sick Pool January 1st of each year. 
 
 
The Sick Bank and Sick Pool are to be administered by the Sick Bank Board.   This Board 
will consist of the Chief of Police, the City Human Resources Director and the President of the 
Police Protective Association or his designee. 
 
 
Policies and procedures for administering the Sick Bank and Sick Pool are to be made by 
the Sick Bank Board, including renewing depleted balances.  These policies and procedures are to 
be in writing and available, upon request, to all sworn officers.  Request for Sick Bank/Sick Pool 
hours are to be made in writing to the Sick Bank Board, in accordance with their policies and 

 
 
4 
procedures.  All requests for Sick Bank/Sick Pool must be approved by the Board prior to use of 
those days. 
 
6. 
VACATION. 
 
 
Each officer shall be entitled to a vacation of eighty-eight hours after one year of 
continuous service, and shall be entitled to an additional eight hours of vacation for each year of 
uninterrupted service provided that an officer is entitled to earn no more than 216 hours of vacation 
in any one year.  Insofar as possible, vacation time shall be determined by division and squad, with 
seniority rank having preference within each division and squad. 
 
 
Not more than 296 hours of accumulated vacation leave can be carried from one calendar 
year to the next calendar year.  Accumulated vacation leave in excess of 296 hours on January 1 
of each year shall be forfeited.   
 
 
On separation from service, an officer shall be paid for all accumulated vacation hours 
earned and not taken, including the year of separation, proportionate to the time employed in that 
year. 
 
7. 
PERSONAL DAY. 
 
 
Each full-time uniformed police officer with one year of continuous service will be 
awarded one personal day each fiscal year.  A personal day shall be defined as one scheduled 
workday. 
 
 
This personal day may be used by the employee at his or her discretion, with supervisory 
approval.  Personal days must be used in the year in which they are awarded.  Personal days cannot 
be broken into increments of less than a normal working day.  They do not carry over from one 
year to the next. 
 
8. 
REDUCTION IN FORCE. 
 
 
Officers laid off in accordance with the provisions of this section will be eligible for rehire 
or reinstatement into a position of the same classification in the inverse order of layoff.  If rehired 
or reinstated, however, no officer shall accumulate time in service during the period of lay off.  An 
officer laid off under these provisions shall be protected for a period of twenty-four months from 
time of separation, pursuant to state statute.  The department shall notify protected officers of any 
vacancy as soon as known to the department.  The officer shall notify the department with-in five 
days of his or her receipt of notice of his or her intent and must report at least fifteen days after 
receipt of notice. 
 
9. 
CLOTHING AND EQUIPMENT ALLOWANCES. 
 
a. Detectives shall be entitled to $60.00 per month clothing allowance. 
 
b. The department will provide all employees with the following equipment: 

 
 
5 
 
 
 
i. 
Uniforms; 
 
 
 
ii. 
Ballistic vest; 
 
 
 
iii. 
Leather gear; 
 
 
 
iv. 
Handcuffs; 
 
 
 
v. 
Baton; 
 
 
 
vi. 
Duty t-shirts; 
 
 
 
vii. 
Handgun; and 
 
 
 
viii. 
Duty footwear. 
 
10. 
MEDICAL AND LIFE INSURANCE. 
 
 
All eligible commissioned officers may elect to participate in any of the health insurance 
plans offered by insurance companies contracted by the City to provide medical and life insurance. 
 
11. 
EXTENSION OF MEDICAL COVERAGE. 
 
 
a. 
All retirees may elect to participate in any of the health insurance plans offered by 
the City’s health insurance provider.  Premiums will be paid by retirees. 
 
 
b. 
If an officer sustains an injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi) in the line of 
duty for the Cheyenne Police Department and is awarded permanent total disability benefits as 
defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xvi), or if an officer dies as a result of an injury as defined  in W.S. 
§ 27-14-102(a)(xi) sustained in the line of duty, the officer and his or her eligible dependents shall 
be offered health insurance at the expense of the City subject to the following terms and conditions: 
 
i. 
The City’s obligation to provide health insurance coverage for the officer 
shall terminate when the officer becomes eligible for coverage under Medicare or 
Medicaid; 
 
ii. 
The City’s obligation to provide health insurance for the officer’s spouse 
shall terminate when the spouse re-marries, becomes eligible for health insurance coverage 
under any employer-sponsored health insurance program, or becomes eligible for Medicare 
or Medicaid, whichever occurs first; and 
 
iii. 
The City’s obligation to provide health insurance coverage for an eligible 
dependent of the officer shall terminate when that dependent reaches the age of twenty-six 
years, or becomes eligible for Medicare or Medicaid, whichever first occurs. 
 

 
 
6 
 
c. 
For the purposes of this paragraph (¶ 11), the word “spouse” shall refer only to the 
person to whom the officer is legally married at the time the officer is awarded permanent total 
disability benefits as defined by W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xvi) and the phrase “eligible dependent” 
shall refer only to a child of the officer, including any child adopted by the officer before the child 
reached the age of fourteen years.  Step-children, foster children, and wards of the officer under 
any guardianship or conservatorship may be eligible dependents for coverage under the plan 
documents for health insurance coverage provided by the City.  
 
 
In the case of a qualifying event, the eligible person may change his or her election within 
thirty days of said qualifying event or wait until open enrollment. 
 
 
The City of Cheyenne may from time to time require verification of permanent disability 
of member, of full time student status or mental or physical disability. 
 
12. 
PPA BUSINESS. 
 
 
With prior immediate supervisor and division head approval, officers may conduct PPA 
business during working hours. 
 
13. 
DUES FOR MEMBERSHIP. 
 
 
The dues for membership in the PPA shall be set forth by the PPA by-laws.  The City 
agrees to deduct this sum, as a payroll deduction, from such member’s monthly paycheck and 
remit these dues to the PPA. 
 
14. 
NEW LANGUAGE. 
 
 
All proposed changes to the wage and benefit policy will be distributed in writing a 
minimum of thirty days prior to the effective date.  The staff officers of the department will be 
available to discuss and explain and take any comments relative to the proposed change.  All other 
policies will be distributed in writing and once each year updated in this manual. 
 
 

 
 
1

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_79997549.pdf
 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 2.08.060, CITY EMPLOYEES 
AND IMMEDIATE FAMILY MEMBERS; CONFLICT OF INTEREST 
POLICY, OF CHAPTER 2.08, CITY OFFICERS AND EMPLOYEES 
GENERALLY, OF TITLE 2, ADMINISTRATION AND PERSONNEL, OF 
THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, 
REQUIRING THE PROMULGATION OF A CONFLICT OF INTEREST 
POLICY FOR EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AS 
SPECIFIED AND MAKING A CONFORMING AMENDMENT TO 
SECTION 2.52.010, REGULATIONS AND BENEFITS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 2.08.060, City Employees and Immediate Family Members; 
Conflict of Interest Policy, of Chapter 2.08, City Officers and Employees Generally, of Title 2, 
Administration and Personnel, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created as set out in 
this ordinance. 
 
2.08.060 – City employees and immediate family members; conflict of interest policy. 
 
A. In accordance with W.S. 15-1-127(c), each employee of the city and any member of 
his or her immediate family shall be governed by the conflict of interest policy specified 
under this section. The human resources department for the city of Cheyenne shall 
consult with the city attorney and other appropriate parties as designated by the director 
of the department and adopt and promulgate the policy. The purpose of the conflict of 
interest policy required under this section shall be to recognize that every city employee 
should seek to prevent conflicts between private interests and public duties, and to 
foster integrity in public service and promote trust and confidence by the general public 
in that service. The policy required under this section shall include provisions 
governing: 
 
1. Financial relationships between an employee of the city or any member of his or 
her immediate family and any outside party that could provide the outside party a 
financial benefit that is only available due to the relationship; 
 
2. Circumstances whereby employees with power or influence over city business or 
decision making could unduly favor relatives, friends or associates with 
employment opportunities with the city of Cheyenne or more favorable supervisory 
conditions if the relative, friend or associate is currently employed by the city; 
 
3. The acceptance of gifts by city employees, including establishing a maximum 
monetary gift value threshold not to exceed fifty dollars ($50.00); 
 
2:30 pm, Jul 17, 2023

 
Page 2 of 3 
 
4. The ability of a city employee to obtain secondary employment outside his or her 
employment with the city of Cheyenne, and if permitted, provisions governing 
reporting requirements for employees who have obtained secondary employment 
outside of the city of Cheyenne; 
 
5. The administration of a reporting process whereby employees may submit concerns 
or complaints regarding potential conflicts of interest of an employee of the city or 
any member of his or her immediate family. The reporting process required under 
this paragraph shall implement mechanisms to combat retaliation against any 
employee submitting a conflict of interest concern or complaint; 
 
6. Any necessary and appropriate exemptions whereby an employee or any member 
of his or her immediate family may not be subject to the requirements of the conflict 
of interest policy required under this section; and 
 
7. Other circumstances as specified by the director of the human resources department 
in consultation with the city attorney and other appropriate parties as designated by 
the director. 
 
B. The director of the human resources department for the city of Cheyenne shall make 
the conflict of interest policy required under this section available to all employees of 
the city upon request and publish the policy on the city's website. 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.52.010, Regulations and Benefits, of Chapter 2.52, Personnel 
System, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
amended as set out in this section. 
 
2.52.010 - Regulations and benefits. 
 
Unless otherwise provided in accordance with Section 2.08.060 of the city code or by law or 
contract, officers and employees of the city are governed by the city personnel (employee) rules 
and regulations on file in the offices of the human resources department and the city clerk. 
 
A. Changes to the city personnel (employee) rules and regulations shall be in 
accordance with the Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act, Wyo. Stat. Sections 
16-3-101, et seq. 
 
B. Any employee appeal from administrative decisions regarding city personnel 
(employee) rules and regulations must be filed with and pursuant to established 
procedures of Chapter 2.92, Administrative Hearing Procedure. This procedure 
does not apply to grievance procedures prior to an appeal. 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 

 
Page 3 of 3 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_91206042.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Lonnie Olson, Planner I 
Subject: An Ordinance Creating a New Section 15.50.070, Neglected Structure, of Chapter 15.50, 
Historic Design Guideline, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Municipal Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Date: 
July 19, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Municipal Code Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
 
07/24/2023 
• 
Public Service Committee  
 
08/08/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
 
08/14/2023 
• 
Public Service Committee  
 
08/22/2023 
• 
Third and final reading   
 
08/28/2023 
 
 
Summary:  
 
The Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board over the last two years has worked on creating a demolition 
by neglect ordinance that seeks to have owners of historic properties maintain their properties and not 
allow them to fall into disrepair. The requirements of the ordinance only apply to structures that have a 
contributing status. A ‘contributing structure’ means a building, structure, site, feature, or object found 
in local or National Register historic district that contributes to the historic character of the district. 
 
The Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board presented the proposed ordinance at a City Council Work 
Session in February of 2021, a public forum in March of 2021, and a Downtown Development Authority 
Work Session in April of 2021. Following those meetings, the Historic Preservation Board met with the 
Planning and Development Department, City Attorney’s Office, and the Building Department to discuss 
the feedback from the meetings listed above and to make comments on the ordinance. The ordinance 
was then edited by the Historic Preservation Board to incorporate the feedback received. Following 
those edits the ordinance was given to the Planning and Development Director and Chief Building 
Official for final revisions.  
 
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN 
ORDINANCE 
CREATING 
A 
NEW 
SECTION 
15.50.070, 
NEGLECTED STRUCTURE, OF CHAPTER 15.50, HISTORIC DESIGN 
GUIDELINE, OF TITLE 15, BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE 
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That a new Section 15.50.070, Neglected Structure, of Chapter 15.50, Historic 
Design Guideline, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby created as set out in this ordinance. 
 
15.50.070 – Neglected structure. 
 
A. Purpose–Authority–General. 
 
1. Owners of certain historic properties are required to maintain their properties and 
not allow them to fall into disrepair and to prevent gradual deterioration. The 
requirements of this section are applicable only to certain properties, termed 
"contributing structures" in this section. That term as used in this section is defined 
to include designated historic structures and properties identified as "contributing" 
in designated historic districts or individually listed. 
 
2. It is a further purpose of this article to protect the health, safety, and welfare of the 
citizens of the city by reducing the risks associated with neglected and deteriorating 
buildings or structures, especially during the process of gradual deterioration. 
 
3. It is the further purpose of this section to provide a just, equitable, and practicable 
method to require the owner of record of the real property on which the qualifying 
building is located or other person or persons who may have legal possession, 
custody, or control of such building or structure (hereinafter the "owner") to 
commence and complete necessary corrective action to repair and stabilize the 
qualifying building and to authorize the city to perform such corrective action if the 
owner fails to commence or complete such corrective action and recover the costs 
of such corrective action from the owner.  
 
B. Definitions. 
 
1. "Abate" shall mean to bring into compliance, halt, eliminate, or remedy. 
 
2. "Agent" means and includes any person acting on behalf of or in place of a property 
owner, including tenant, manager, or lessee. 
 
6:43 pm, Jul 14 2023

3. "Neglected Structure" means imminent total or partial destruction of or damage to 
a historic structure or any portion thereof, due to the failure of the owner(s) to 
adequately maintain or repair the structure, excepting acts of God or natural or 
manmade disasters. Exposure to the elements and seasonal weather shall not be 
considered an act of God or natural disaster. 
 
4. "Director" means the Planning and Development Director or their designee. 
 
5. "Hearing Officer" means an independent hearing officer appointed by the mayor 
and confirmed by the city council in accordance with Chapter 2.92 of this code. 
 
6. "Historic structure" means all structures contributing to the city's Contributing 
Structures List and structures listed on the National Register of Historic Buildings. 
 
7. "Maintenance and repair" means any work, for which a building permit is not 
required by law, where the purpose and effect of such work is to correct any 
deterioration, decay, or damage to a structure or any architecturally significant part 
thereof and to restore or replace, as nearly as may be practicable, to its condition 
prior to the occurrence of such deterioration, decay, or damage. 
 
8. "Owner" means and includes: 
 
a. Any owner or holder of any legal or equitable estate in real property, including 
a dominant or servient tenement, the interest of a public trustee, lienholder, 
mortgagee, or beneficiary of a deed of trust; or 
 
b. The owner of record as reflected by the records of the office of the county 
assessor, or county clerk. 
 
9. "Person" means and includes a natural person, association, corporation, partnership, 
limited liability company, and any other legal entity capable of owning, using, or 
occupying real property. 
 
10. "Preservation" means the act or process of applying measures necessary to sustain 
the existing form, integrity, and materials of a contributing structure. 
 
11. "Undue economic hardship" means the inability of the owner and parties in interest 
to obtain a reasonable return or a reasonably beneficial use from the contributing 
structure that is the subject of demolition by neglect, or the inability of the owner 
and parties in interest to finance the required repairs to the contributing structure. 
 
12. "Review Committee" means a group of people that are appointed by the CHPB 
which investigate petitions filed with the Director alleging that a violation this 
Chapter has occurred and recommend to the CHPB if further action shall occur.    
 
C. Maintenance and Major Repairs Requirements. 

 
1. Duty to maintain. Any historic structure shall be preserved against material 
deterioration of exterior features and structural elements, as identified by the factors 
set forth in subsection 3. below, by the owner of such qualifying building, and it 
shall be the duty of the owner to maintain and repair the qualifying building in a 
manner that prevents such deterioration. 
 
2. Duty to repair. The owner of a historic structure shall, upon written notice of 
violation from the city as described herein, repair such exterior features or structural 
elements of the historic structure if any such exterior features or structural elements 
are materially deteriorating or if the condition of any such features or elements is 
contributing to material deterioration of the qualifying building including, but not 
limited to, any of the conditions as listed in subsection 3. below. 
 
3. All historic structures shall be preserved against decay and deterioration as set forth 
by the International Property Maintenance Code adopted by the City of Cheyenne 
Ordinance No. 15.24.010 to prevent being a neglected structure. 
 
D. Monitoring. 
 
1. Any Historical Register nominations or surveys, photographs, and county assessor 
data shall serve as the baseline from which any decay and deterioration outlined in 
subsection C.3. above is determined. For the purposes of this section, completed 
plans not approved by the city shall be allowed to serve as a baseline.   
 
E. Process for Submitting a Petition Alleging Neglected Structure. 
 
1. The initial determination that there is a condition of neglect shall be made by the 
Director after an investigation that is initiated by a petition from any person who is 
familiar with the subject property, which may include, but not be limited to, a city 
employee, the Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board, or any citizen. 
 
2. Filing of Petition. 
 
a. Petitions charging that a structure is a neglected structure, shall be filed with 
the Director. The city may charge a reasonable fee to cover costs associated 
with investigation, processing, and research regarding the petition. Fees shall 
be set administratively by the city and shall be made available to the public. 
 
b. The Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board (CHPB) may file a petition on its 
own initiative. Petitions by the CHPB shall be exempt from fees. 
 
c. Any official, commission, or department of the city, any state agency, or any 
local or state historical, preservation, or business association may request in 
writing to the CHPB that it make a preliminary investigation of a structure to 
determine whether a basis exists for a determination of neglected structure. 

Such investigation shall be undertaken by the review committee as per 
subsection E.3. below. 
 
d. Petitions shall be filed in a format determined by the city to clearly describe and 
illustrate the specific defects citing in each instance the specific standard or 
standards (as outlined in subsection C.3.) being violated. 
 
e. A petition alleging a neglected structure may not be filed for the same property 
more frequently than once every one (1) year. 
 
3. Whenever a petition is filed with the Director by either the CHPB, resident of 
the city, or city building inspector charging that a historic structure is threatened 
with a neglected structure as set out in subsection C.3., such charge shall be 
referred by the Director to the review committee for review.  The review 
committee shall make a preliminary investigation and finding of the charge 
within one-hundred eighty (180) days of the filing of the petition, applying the 
standards set forth in section C. Each review committee member shall conduct 
their preliminary investigations independently.  
 
4. The CHPB shall discuss the petition and the review committee's findings at the 
next regularly scheduled CHPB meeting after conclusion of the preliminary 
investigation. A finding of a violation of this Chapter must be made by motion 
and passed by a majority of the members of Board.  If the CHPB finds a 
violation of this Chapter, the CHPB shall issue and cause to be served upon the 
owner and parties in interest of the subject property a written complaint stating 
the charges the CHPB finds has a basis for a neglected structure, including a 
copy of the petition and this section of the Cheyenne city code and a notice that 
a hearing may be held before the Director pursuant to subsection F.1. below. 
 
 
F. Process for Notifying the Property Owner. 
 
1. On receipt of a petition, the Director shall notify the owners in writing of the 
allegation and the process for making a decision regarding the petition, 
including any applicable deadlines. The notice shall, among other things: 
 
a. Offer the owner the opportunity to meet in person with the Director to 
present any relevant information. 
 
b. Notify the owner that the owner shall have thirty (30) days to comply by 
commencing and diligently pursuing repairs as directed. Unless extended 
pursuant to the provisions of subsection I., such repairs must be completed 
no later than one hundred eighty (180) days from the date the notice is 
received by the owner(s). 
 
c. Notify the owner that the owner may appeal the complaint by filing a written 
appeal in the office of the city clerk in accordance with the city's 

administrative hearing process in Chapter 2.92 within ten (10) days of 
receipt of the notice according to subsection F.  If no appeal is filed within 
the ten (10) day period, the complaint shall become final which shall not be 
subject to further review.  
 
d. Notify the owner of their right to a hearing as outlined in  
subsection I.1.  
 
2. Notice shall be delivered by personal service or first class mail, and shall be 
considered effective if such mail is not returned by the post office within fifteen 
(15) days of mailing. In case of notice by first class mail, notice shall also be 
posted on the property.  
 
3. If the whereabouts of such persons are unknown and the same cannot be 
ascertained by the Director in the exercise of reasonable diligence, then the 
serving of such notice may be made by publishing the same once each week for 
two (2) successive weeks in a newspaper or online news publication generally 
circulated within the city. Where such service is by publication, a notice of the 
pending proceedings shall be posted in a conspicuous place on the premises 
thereby affected. 
 
G. Suspension of Process.  The above process may be suspended in the event the owner 
agrees in writing to correct a condition of neglect resulting in a complaint within a time 
period determined to be reasonable by the Director, or designee. If the condition is not 
corrected within that time period, the process shall continue where it was suspended. 
 
H. Compliance. 
 
1. If, pursuant to subsections E.3 and E.4, the review committee determines a 
neglected structure has occurred and issues a complaint, the owner shall 
commence with any and all repairs that are necessary to satisfy the complaint. 
Such repairs must be completed no later than one hundred eighty (180) days 
from the date the notice is received by the owner unless a written request for an 
extension is submitted to and approved by the Director prior to the running of 
the one hundred eighty (180) day period. Absent extraordinary circumstances, 
as determined in the sole discretion of the Director no extension shall be for 
more than ninety (90) additional days. 
 
2. If, as determined by the Director the owner complies with the complaint by 
commencing and diligently pursuing and completing the repair of the violations 
as directed within the specified time, the Director will take no further action at 
that time in regard to the notice or the final notice and the owner will not be 
subject to the remedies and penalties provided herein. 
 
I. Right to Hearing. 
 

1. Pursuant to subsection F(1)(c), if CHPB's written determination indicates a 
basis for a neglected structure, within fourteen (14) days of the determination 
of the CHPB, the Director shall issue and cause to be served upon the property 
owners, as may be determined by reasonable diligence, a complaint stating the 
charges in that respect, including a copy of the petition and this section of the 
Cheyenne city code. The complaint shall contain a notice that, should the 
property owner appeal the complaint pursuant to the city's administrative 
hearing process in Chapter 2.92. and that a hearing may be held at the property 
owner's written request before the city's hearing officer no less than thirty (30) 
days nor more than forty-five (45) days after the serving of such complaint; that 
the property owners shall be given a right to testimony at the place and time 
fixed in the complaint.  The chair of the CHPB shall also be given notice of the 
hearing. 
 
2. The purpose of the hearing is to receive evidence concerning the charge of 
deterioration and to ascertain whether the property owner wishes to make a 
claim of undue economic hardship. 
 
3. Right to a hearing: 
 
a. Regarding allegation of lack of compliance.  A hearing may be held by the 
hearing officer to determine if the owner has failed to commence, diligently 
pursue, or complete the repairs of the violations within the time specified in 
the notice or final notice, as applicable.  Such hearing shall be public, and 
the owner shall have a right to file a response to the allegation prior to the 
public hearing. The hearing shall be subject to the procedural rules of the 
administrative hearing process in Chapter 2.92. 
 
b. Determination Neglected Structure. If the property owner disagrees with the 
determination that the subject property is in a "Neglected Structure", the 
property owner shall have a right to appeal that determination in accordance 
with the rules of this section. Such hearing shall be public, and the owner 
shall have a right to file a response to the allegation prior to the public 
hearing. The hearing shall be subject to the procedural rules of the 
administrative hearing process in Chapter 2.92. 
 
c. Determination of Undue Economic Hardship. Determinations made by the 
CHPB and the Plan for Relief of Economic Hardship prepared by the CHPB 
may be appealed to the hearing officer. To perfect such an appeal, the appeal 
must be filed by an aggrieved party pursuant to the city's administrative 
hearing process in Chapter 2.92 within ten (10) days following receipt of 
the determination or the order for repair of the property. 
 
4. A record of the entire hearing shall be made by tape recording or by other means 
of permanent recording determined appropriate. A recording of the proceedings 
shall be made available to all parties upon request and upon payment of a fee 

established by the city. 
 
5. Oral evidence shall be taken only on oath or affirmation.  
 
6. Irrelevant and unduly repetitious evidence shall be excluded. 
 
7. Each party shall have the following rights, among others: 
 
a. To call and examine witnesses on any matter relevant to the issues of the 
hearing; 
 
b. To introduce documentary and physical evidence; 
 
c. To cross examine opposing witnesses on any matter relevant to the issue of 
the hearing; 
 
d. To refuse the evidence against the party; and 
 
e. To representation by counsel.  
 
 
J. Economic Hardship. 
 
1. The property owner is entitled to make a claim of undue economic hardship if 
the owner claims to be unable to make needed repairs to the property because 
it is economically unfeasible. Enforcement of the review committee's 
determination shall be stayed until after the CHPB's determination regarding 
the claim. 
 
2. When a claim of economic hardship owning to the effects of this section is made 
in writing by the owner within fifteen (15) days of service of the notice of 
complaint, the CHPB shall schedule a hearing on the claim to be held within 
thirty (30) days of receipt of a claim of economic hardship. For the purposes of 
this section, when a claim of economic hardship is made, the owner must prove 
the following: 
 
a. The qualifying building is incapable of earning a reasonable return, 
regardless of whether that return represents the most profitable return 
possible; 
 
b. The qualifying building cannot be adapted for any other use, whether by the 
current owner or by a purchaser, which would result in a reasonable return; 
and qualifying building and preserving it have failed; 
 
c. Diligent efforts to find a purchaser interested in acquiring the neglected 
structure. 
 

3. Findings of economic hardship. Based on the information presented at the 
hearing, and within no more than thirty (30) days following, the CHPB shall 
make a finding of economic hardship or no economic hardship and shall enter 
the reasons for such finding into the record. 
 
a. If a finding of no economic hardship is made, the CHPB shall serve upon 
the owner or the owner's attorney a final notice ordering the owner to make 
repairs, improvements, or correction of defects pursuant to subsection I.1. 
In the event of a finding of undue economic hardship, the finding shall be 
accompanied by a recommended plan to relieve the economic hardship. 
 
4. Plan to Relieve Economic Hardship. 
 
a. A recommended plan to relieve the economic hardship shall include, but is 
not limited to: 
 
i. Loans or grants from the city, the county, or other public, private, or 
nonprofit sources; 
 
ii. Acquisition by purchase; 
 
iii. Changes in building code modifications, applicable zoning regulations, 
or relaxation of the provisions of this section sufficient to mitigate the 
undue economic hardship; and 
 
iv. Capital expenditure by the property owner. 
 
b. The CHPB shall not require the owner to perform and the city shall not 
perform any repairs the cost of which will exceed the value, as appraised 
for property tax purposes, of the qualifying building and land on which it is 
located.  
 
c. The Director shall issue an order regarding the time period during which the 
property should be repaired, improved, or defects corrected, taking into 
account the provisions of the recommended plan. 
 
5. Other City Powers; City's Election of Remedies. 
 
a. Nothing contained within this section shall diminish the city's power to 
declare a building unsafe or in violation of the minimum housing code or 
any other applicable statute or code. In addition, the procedures described 
herein are mandatory only for determinations being made solely under the 
authority of this section. Where other sections of the Cheyenne city code 
apply, the city may, in its discretion, choose to process any action regarding 
the property under such other provisions alone, or under such provisions 
along with these provisions concurrently, or solely under these provisions. 

The city may also suspend the procedures of this section at any time if an 
action has been initiated under other applicable law. 
 
 
K. Enforcement Provision. 
 
1. Penalties and Remedies. 
 
a. Enforcement of this section shall be any one or more of the following 
methods, and the institution of any action under any of these methods shall 
not relieve any party from any other civil or criminal proceeding prescribed 
for violations and prohibitions. 
 
i. Equitable Remedy. The city may apply for any appropriate equitable 
remedy to enforce the provisions of this section. 
 
ii. Order for Compliance.  The city can apply for and the court may issue 
an order directing that improvements or repairs be made or that any 
other action be taken that is necessary to bring the property into 
compliance with this section. Whenever the party is cited for contempt 
by the court and the city has executed the order of abatement, the city 
shall have a lien on the property for the cost of executing the order of 
abatement.  
 
iii. Civil Penalty.  Civil penalties can be assessed for failure to comply with 
a final administrative determination or an un-appealed CHPB review 
committee decision under the provisions and guidelines for assessing 
such penalties for zoning code violations. Prior to imposing a civil 
penalty, the Planning and Development Department shall deliver a 
written notice by personal service or by registered mail or by certified 
mail, return receipt requested, to the person responsible for the violation 
indicating the nature of the violation and ordering corrective action. 
Where the violation is the failure to remedy a condition of neglect within 
the time periods provided by the Director or the CHPB, no additional 
time period for compliance need be given. The notice shall include 
information regarding the possible assessment of civil penalties and 
other possible enforcement actions. If this notice is appealed to the 
BOA, the CHPB shall not rehear any issue that was heard by the CHPB 
or could have been so heard had an appeal to the CHPB been made. 
Rather, the BOA shall limit the scope of its review to whether there has 
been compliance with the Director's, or designee's, determination or the 
CHPB's determination, as applicable. 
 
2. Enforcement Alternatives. 
 
a. In addition to the penalties authorized by subsection M.1., a violation which 
is intentional, or occurs through gross negligence, shall be subject to the 

following provisions: 
 
i. No permit, administrative approval, or land use application relating to 
the real property, or the entity owning the property, which is the subject 
of the alleged violation shall be issued under Title 15 of the Cheyenne 
city code for any alteration or new construction affecting such property 
for a period of five (5) years, or if ownership changes, following the last 
date of the violation, other than permits necessary to correct the 
violation. However, upon presentation of evidence satisfactory to the 
CHPB that the violation has been corrected, any remaining portion of 
the five (5) year prohibition on issuance of a permit may be waived. 
 
3. Enforcement. If the owner refuses or fails to commence, diligently pursue, and 
complete the necessary repairs within the time period as specified in the final 
notice or final order, the city is hereby authorized to have performed and to 
complete the necessary remedial work to prevent further deterioration and to 
defray the cost thereof as provided herein. 
 
4. Expenditures for Repair Work. The city may elect to pay for the repair work 
and charge to the owner of such qualifying building the expenses incurred in 
doing such work or having such work done. If such work is performed at the 
expense of the city, such expenses shall be assessed as a lien on the real estate, 
lot, or lots improved by the qualifying building where the expense incurred. 
Subject to compliance with applicable law, the city and its authorized assistants, 
employees, agents, or other representatives are hereby expressly authorized to 
enter upon private property at all reasonable hours for the purpose of 
completing repairs necessary for the repair of a qualifying building. The 
Planning and Development Department shall have the authority to issue all 
orders necessary to enforce this provision. 
 
5. Establishment of Lien. The city, or an agent authorized to act on behalf of the 
city, shall file a statement of such expenses eligible for recording by filing the 
amount of such expenses and the date on which the work was done with the 
register's office of the county. The city shall have a mechanic's or materialman's 
lien on such real estate, lot, or lots improved by the qualifying building upon 
which the work was done to secure the expenditures. The lien amount shall bear 
a ten (10) percent interest per annum from the date that the statement was filed, 
or up to the amount allowed by law. For any such expenditures and interest, suit 
may be instituted and recovery and foreclosure of the lien may be had in the 
name of the city against the owner. The statement of expenses shall be prima 
facie proof of the amount expended for such work or improvements. The county 
tax collector shall collect these costs at the same time and in the same manner 
as property taxes are collected and shall include penalties and interest calculated 
as if it were overdue property taxes. However, nothing in this section shall be 
construed to impair or limit in any way the power of the city or county to define 
and declare nuisances and to cause their removal or abatement by summary 

proceedings or otherwise. 
 
a. Each day during which any person commits, or allows to remain unabated, 
any of the actions specified as unlawful in this section shall constitute a 
separate offense. Multiple violations of this section may be included on a 
single notice to abate or a single summons and complaint. 
 
6. Release of Lien. The lien granted by this section shall be extinguished and 
released upon the payment to the city of all amounts owing hereunder, upon a 
finding that the lien was placed in error, or by the operation of law. The lien 
granted by this section may also be forgiven and released by agreement upon a 
showing, by a prospective purchaser for value of the real property encumbered 
by a lien or liens, that the aggregate value of such liens against the property 
exceeds the appraised value of the property and that the purchaser will purchase 
the lot if the liens are forgiven or reduced. Such action shall be at the sole 
discretion of the Planning and Development Department and the city attorney's 
office, subject to the approval of the CHPB, it shall be the burden of any person 
seeking the forgiveness or reduction of any such liens to prove to the 
satisfaction of the Planning and Development Department that the applicable 
conditions have been satisfied. 
 
 
Section 2.  That the current Section 15.50.070, Appeal, is renumbered to Section 
15.50.080, and that the current Section 15.50.080, Violation–Penalty, is renumbered to Section 
15.50.090. 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_50966911.pdf
 
 
ORDINANCE NO.  _________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE SUBMITTING TO THE QUALIFIED PROPERTY 
OWNERS OF REAL PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE 
CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, EXCLUDING REAL PROPERTY USED 
EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, AT A SPECIAL 
ELECTION ON WEDNESDAY, OCTOBER 18, 2023, THE FOLLOWING 
QUESTION: 'SHALL A SPECIAL ASSESSMENT OF TWENTY (20) 
MILLS BE LEVIED UPON THE ASSESSED VALUE OF REAL 
PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE CHEYENNE 
DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT, EXCLUDING 
REAL 
PROPERTY 
USED 
EXCLUSIVELY 
FOR 
RESIDENTIAL 
PURPOSES, FOR A PERIOD OF FOUR (4) YEARS PURSUANT TO THE 
PROVISIONS OF WYO. STAT. § 15-9-217(b)  FOR THE PURPOSES OF 
(1) LANDSCAPING 
OR 
MAINTENANCE 
OF 
PUBLIC 
AREAS; 
(2) PLANNING 
OR 
MANAGEMENT 
OF 
DEVELOPMENT 
OR 
IMPROVEMENT ACTIVITIES; (3) PROMOTION OF PUBLIC EVENTS; 
AND (4) ACTIVITIES IN SUPPORT OF BUSINESS RECRUITMENT AND 
DEVELOPMENT?'." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the governing body of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, by 
Resolution No. 6342 adopted July 24, 2023, has authorized the holding of a special election as 
provided by Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-217(b), which, by a majority of persons voting in the election, 
owning real property within the downtown development authority excluding real property used 
exclusively for residential purposes may authorize a special assessment of twenty (20) mills 
against the assessed value of real property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown 
Development Authority District, Cheyenne, Wyoming, excluding real property used exclusively 
for residential purposes. 
 
Section 2.  That the special assessment shall expire in four (4) years from the effective date 
of the assessment unless re-authorized by the governing body and a majority of persons voting in 
the election, owning certain real property within the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority 
District, at a subsequent election. The assessment shall be levied and collected in the same manner 
as the general property tax levy for the municipality. 
 
Section 3.  That the established boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development 
Authority District are as follows: 
 
Northern Portion:  Commencing at a point described as the intersection of the west 
right-of-way of House Avenue and the south right-of-way of 22nd Street; thence in 
a southwesterly direction along the south right-of-way line to a point intersecting 
2:49 pm, Aug 01, 2023

 
 
with the east right-of-way of Snyder Avenue; thence in a southeasterly direction 
along the east right-of-way line and along the extension of that line to a point which 
intersects with a line that is 17.2 feet parallel to the center line of the northern most 
rail of the Union Pacific Railroad; thence in a northeasterly direction along said 
parallel line to a point intersecting with the extension of the west right-of-way line 
of House Avenue; thence in a northwesterly direction along the west right-of-way 
line of House Avenue to the point of beginning. 
 
Southern Portion:  Commencing at a point described as the intersection of the west 
right-of-way line of Central Avenue and the south right-of-way line of 11th Street; 
thence southwesterly along the south right-of-way of 11th Street to a point 
intersecting the east right-of-way line of Capitol Avenue; thence southeasterly 
along the east right-of-way line and that line extends to a point intersecting the 
constructed center line of Deming Drive; thence southeasterly along the center line 
of Deming Drive to the extension of the right-of-way line of Central Avenue; thence 
northwesterly along the west right-of-way line of Central Avenue to the point of 
beginning. 
 
Section 4.  That at a special election to be held in Cheyenne, Wyoming on Wednesday, 
October 18, 2023, there shall be submitted to the qualified electors owning certain real property 
within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District, the question 
of authorizing a special assessment of twenty (20) mills to be levied as more particularly set forth 
in the Proclamation and Notice of Election. 
 
Section 5.  That the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District shall be 
considered as one election precinct for the special election on the question of authorizing a special 
assessment of twenty (20) mills, and the sole voting site shall be located in the lobby of the Historic 
Union Pacific Depot Building, 121 West 15th Street. On the day of the election the polling place 
shall open at 9:00 a.m. and shall remain open continuously until 3:00 p.m. at which time the polling 
place shall be closed. 
 
Section 6.  That qualified electors as outlined in the Proclamation and Notice of Election 
shall be entitled to vote on the question in person or by absentee ballot as provided by special 
election procedures and guidelines as adopted by the governing body. A qualified elector includes 
persons owning real property within the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District, 
excluding real property used exclusively for residential purposes, or those persons obligated for 
the payment of general property taxes for said property within the District pursuant to property tax 
notices issued by the Laramie County Assessor or the Wyoming Department of Revenue. 
 
 
 
The Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority shall request the Laramie County 
Assessor to compile a list of qualified property owners, including those persons obligated for the 
payment of general property taxes for the properties affected by the assessment, and provide the 
same to the City Clerk for purposes of compiling a list of qualified electors. 
 

 
 
 
Absentee voting on the question is permitted as hereinafter provided for in the 
Proclamation and Notice of Election. 
 
Section 7.  That paper ballots shall be used for voting, including absentee voting, and 
tabulating of votes cast "for" and "against" the ballot question. The ballots shall set forth the 
following question: 
 
"SHALL A SPECIAL ASSESSMENT OF TWENTY (20) MILLS BE LEVIED UPON THE 
ASSESSED VALUE OF REAL PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE 
CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT, EXCLUDING 
REAL PROPERTY USED EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, FOR A PERIOD 
OF FOUR (4) YEARS PURSUANT TO THE PROVISIONS OF WYO. STAT. § 15-9-217(b) 
FOR THE PURPOSES OF (1) LANDSCAPING OR MAINTENANCE OF PUBLIC AREAS; (2) 
PLANNING OR MANAGEMENT OF DEVELOPMENT OR IMPROVEMENT ACTIVITIES; 
(3) PROMOTION OF PUBLIC EVENTS; AND (4) ACTIVITIES IN SUPPORT OF BUSINESS 
RECRUITMENT AND DEVELOPMENT?"  
 
 
The paper ballots are to be marked by the voter to record either "for" or "against" the ballot 
question. Paper ballots and absentee paper ballots shall be tabulated by manual count by the 
Canvassing Board following closure of the polling place. 
 
Section 8.  That approval of this ordinance shall be not more than forty-five (45) nor less 
than thirty (30) days before the date of the election. In addition to publication of the ordinance, 
including a map of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District boundaries, the City 
Clerk shall cause the Proclamation and Notice of Election and Public Notice of Elector 
Qualifications portion of the ordinance, including the map of the District, to be published twice in 
a newspaper of general circulation within the City prior to the election date. The Proclamation and 
Notice shall specify the name of the City, the date, time and place of election, the purpose of the 
election and the fact that only qualified electors shall be permitted to vote thereon. The 
Proclamation and Notice of Election and Public Notice of Elector Qualifications, including an 
Affidavit and Qualification of Elector form, shall be in substantially the following form: 
 
PROCLAMATION AND NOTICE OF SPECIAL 
CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT ELECTION 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, has approved Ordinance No. ______, the title of which Ordinance is as follows: 
 
ORDINANCE NO. _________          
 
"AN ORDINANCE SUBMITTING TO THE QUALIFIED PROPERTY OWNERS OF REAL 
PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN 
DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, EXCLUDING 
REAL PROPERTY USED EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, AT A SPECIAL 

 
 
ELECTION ON WEDNESDAY, OCTOBER 18, 2023, THE FOLLOWING QUESTION: 
'SHALL A SPECIAL ASSESSMENT OF TWENTY (20) MILLS BE LEVIED UPON THE 
ASSESSED VALUE OF REAL PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE 
CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT, EXCLUDING 
REAL PROPERTY USED EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, FOR A PERIOD 
OF FOUR (4) YEARS PURSUANT TO THE PROVISIONS OF WYO. STAT. § 15-9-217(b)  
FOR THE PURPOSES OF (1) LANDSCAPING OR MAINTENANCE OF PUBLIC AREAS; (2) 
PLANNING OR MANAGEMENT OF DEVELOPMENT OR IMPROVEMENT ACTIVITIES; 
(3) PROMOTION OF PUBLIC EVENTS; AND (4) ACTIVITIES IN SUPPORT OF BUSINESS 
RECRUITMENT AND DEVELOPMENT?'." 
 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN that at a special election to be held on Wednesday, 
October 18, 2023, between the hours of 9:00 a.m. and 3:00 p.m., there shall be submitted to 
qualified electors the following question: 
 
"SHALL A SPECIAL ASSESSMENT OF TWENTY (20) MILLS BE LEVIED UPON THE 
ASSESSED VALUE OF REAL PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE 
CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT, EXCLUDING 
REAL PROPERTY USED EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, FOR A PERIOD 
OF FOUR (4) YEARS PURSUANT TO THE PROVISIONS OF WYO. STAT. § 15-9-217(b)  
FOR THE PURPOSES OF (1) LANDSCAPING OR MAINTENANCE OF PUBLIC AREAS; (2) 
PLANNING OR MANAGEMENT OF DEVELOPMENT OR IMPROVEMENT ACTIVITIES; 
(3) PROMOTION OF PUBLIC EVENTS; AND (4) ACTIVITIES IN SUPPORT OF BUSINESS 
RECRUITMENT AND DEVELOPMENT?" 
 
 
The sole Election Polling Place shall be as follows: 
 
Lobby of the Historic Union Pacific Depot Building 
121 West 15th Street 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 
 
 
Any qualified elector shall be entitled to vote on the election question in person or by 
absentee ballot as provided in procedures and guidelines for the special election adopted by the 
governing body. Qualified electors for this election are persons owning real property within the 
Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District, excluding real property used exclusively 
for residential purposes, or those persons obligated to pay property taxes on the property within 
the District affected by the assessment per property tax notices issued by the Laramie County 
Assessor or the Wyoming Department of Revenue. A boundary map of the Downtown 
Development Authority District is on file with City Clerk's Office. 
 
 
 
One vote shall be allocated to each property that receives a separately issued property tax 
assessment notice from the Laramie County Assessor or the Wyoming Department of Revenue.  
The owner or owners of each separately assessed parcel of non-residential real property within the 
boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District shall designate one 
representative to cast the vote allocated to that parcel. 

 
 
 
 
Votes cast on election day shall be manually tabulated, following closure of the polling 
place, by the Canvassing Board at the polling place. The ballots shall contain the same question as 
stated above, and a paper ballot shall be provided to permit the elector to indicate his or her vote 
of "for" or "against" the question. Absentee paper ballots shall be manually tabulated, following 
closure of the polling place, by the Canvassing Board and ballots shall contain the same question 
as stated above. 
 
 
Any qualified elector who will be absent on election day, or who cannot be present at the 
polling place on election day, may vote by absentee ballot upon making application in person, in 
writing, or by telephone, to the City Clerk's Office, 2101 O'Neil Ave., Room 101, Cheyenne, WY 
82001, (307) 637-6329, for an absentee ballot, and by furnishing the following information: 
 
Name in full, name in which the property is held and if held by a legal entity, how 
applicant is associated with the entity; the election for which an absentee ballot is 
requested; the location of the property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne 
Downtown Development Authority District that is owned by the elector; and, if not 
obtained in person, the address to which the absentee ballot is to be mailed. 
 
 
Request for an absentee ballot may be made on behalf of an absentee qualified elector if 
the prescribed information is furnished. Such absentee voter shall be entitled to vote upon 
executing an affidavit, indicating the applicant is entitled to vote in the election, which affidavit 
must be printed on the reverse side of the inner ballot envelope to be returned to the City Clerk. 
 
 
Request for an absentee ballot must be made not later than the close of business on the day 
prior to the election. To be counted, an absentee ballot must be received in the City Clerk's Office 
on or before 3:00 p.m. on the day of the election. 
 
 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the governing body of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, has 
caused this Proclamation and Notice of Special Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority 
District Election to be given by the City Clerk as of the ____ day of ______________, 2023. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
___________________________________  
 
 
 
 
 
            Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

PUBLIC NOTICE OF ELECTOR QUALIFICATIONS 
FOR CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY 
DISTRICT SPECIAL ELECTION 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
 

 
 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN that the list of qualified electors, who are eligible 
to cast their ballot at the District's special election to be held in Cheyenne, Wyoming on 
Wednesday, October 18, 2023, shall be those persons owning real property within the  Cheyenne 
Downtown Development Authority District, excluding real property used exclusively for 
residential purposes, or those persons obligated for the payment of property taxes for the property 
affected by the assessment within the District as provided by property tax assessment notification 
as issued by the Laramie County Assessor or the Wyoming Department of Revenue. 
 
 
Any qualified elector whose name does not appear on the list provided by the Laramie 
County Assessor may vote either in person or by absentee ballot by furnishing his or her 
qualifications to the City Clerk (if voting by absentee ballot) or to the election judges at the polling 
site on the day of the election by execution of an Affidavit and Qualification of Elector in 
substantially the following form:

COUNTY OF LARAMIE

QUALIFICATION OF ELECTOR 
CITY OF CHEYENNE

COMES 
NOW, 
______________________________________________, 
having 
appeared at the CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY DISTRICT 
SPECIAL ELECTION and desiring to vote at said election and being a bona fide property owner 
of real property not used exclusively for residential purposes within said District, or the person 
obligated to pay property taxes for the property affected by the assessment within the District as 
provided by property tax assessment notice as issued by the Laramie County Assessor or the 
Wyoming Department of Revenue, hereby states the following: 
 
___ 
1. 
That I am an owner of real property not used exclusively for residential purposes 
located within the Authority District;  
 
OR 
 
___ 
2. 
I am not the owner, however I am legally obligated for the payment of property 
taxes for real property not used exclusively for residential purposes located within 
the District affected by this assessment and receive property tax assessment notices 
as issued by the Laramie County Assessor or the Wyoming Department of Revenue. 
 
 
I hereby swear (or affirm), under penalty of perjury, that I have the authority to vote in this 
election and that the above information is true and correct. 
 
DATE OF ELECTION: October 18, 2023

PUBLICATION DATE: 
 
 
 
Section 9.  That immediately following closure of the polling place, the Canvassing Board 
shall proceed to tabulate and canvass the votes cast on the ballot question. The results disclosed 
by the canvassing of votes shall be certified by the Canvassing Board to the City Clerk. The 
Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne shall review the Canvassing Board results and officially 
declare the result of the election by adoption of a resolution at a meeting open to the public. 
 
 
 
If the majority of votes cast on the ballot question are in favor of the special assessment, 
the question shall have carried and the governing body, in the manner provided by law, shall 
proceed to declare the results of the special election to the Laramie County Assessor to allow for 
the special assessments to be levied and collected in the same manner as the general property tax 
levy for the municipality. 
 
Section 10.  That the election shall be held, conducted and the results tabulated and 
canvassed as set forth in this ordinance and election procedures and guidelines as approved by 
resolution by the governing body. 
 
Section 11.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.
