Appendix C – Village Center Architectural Standards and Guidelines 
 
Dated: 2/14/2022 
Appendix C: Village Center Architectural Standards & Guidelines 
A. Applicability: These Village Center Architectural Standards and Guidelines (“the Guidelines”) communicate 
design principles and criteria for the Village Center Land Use District. These standards supplement the 
Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development Standards and Guidelines, as amended (“the PUD”). 
B. Architectural Intent: Buildings will express a timeless “Modern Farmhouse” concept to emulate an established 
Town Center organized around a Village Green, with the goal of setting a precedent for future development 
throughout Sweetgrass. In the event of a conflict between the Guidelines and the remainder of the PUD 
document, this document shall prevail. 
C. Process: Plans shall be submitted to the Design Review Committee (DRC) for review of compliance with these 
Guidelines, as well as the PUD and the UDC. As these Guidelines cannot anticipate all scenarios for the 
development and design process, an applicant may propose alternatives and/or substitutions. 
 
To deviate from these Guidelines, the following shall be considered:  
1. Does the deviation benefit the community (e.g., does the proposal include building materials, a design 
approach, and/or technologies that furthers the intent of these Guidelines but not contemplated herein)? 
2. Will there be an undue hardship resulting from complying with the Guidelines (e.g., have building 
materials specified herein or construction methods become outdated and/or cost-prohibitive)? 
If responses to both C.1 and C.2 are affirmative, substitution may be allowed by the Director through the 
processes identified in UDC §2.3.4 – Administrative Adjustment with DRC review and endorsement. 
D. Land Uses: Allowable land uses in the Village Center are described in PUD §4: Table 10. 
E. The Village Green and Pedestrian Connectivity 
1. The Village Green (“the Green”) is a private park, around which the Village Center uses are organized. 
2. Most of the Green will be irrigated bluegrass or similar intended for passive recreation and active listening 
and participation in concerts, plays, and outdoor festivals with appropriate permitting secured from the 
City. As such, full public access to portions of the Green may be restricted from time to time, at the 
Owner’s sole discretion. 
3. The Green shall include a paved and ADA accessible walkway system and allows maintenance vehicular 
access to sanitary sewer manholes and connectivity between the Greenway trail, the Green, and 
associated parking areas. 
4. Pedestrian seating areas, such as benches, planters, seat walls, or other similar features that are not 
associated with a specific business shall be included in the Green and associated spaces between buildings. 
5. Covered walkways, particularly along the Green, are encouraged. 
6. Establishments along façades facing the Green shall connect to the common walkway surrounding the 
Green, such that the primary entry to the business should be facing the Green. 
7. No additional Park and Open Space is required as part of the Village Center. 
F. Building Use, Form and Articulation 
1. Intent: Human scale shall be maintained. Building forms should be understated and simple with secondary 
building forms such as porches, dormers, porticos, chimneys, and balconies incorporated into the overall 
massing to reduce the apparent scale of the building. 
2. Building height, setbacks, separation, and other dimensional standards: per PUD Section 4: Table 9. 
3. Orientation: 
a. References to “front” architecture imply the building façade adjacent to the Green. 
b. Building façades adjacent to the Green will be expected to be more finely textured than those facing 
the periphery, and will include more windows, doors, and detailing at a pedestrian scale. 
 

 
 
Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development 
Page C-2 
Standards & Guidelines (4th Amendment) 
Appendix C – Village Center Architectural Standards and Guidelines 
 
Dated: 2/14/2022 
4. Articulation: 
a. Building plans shall be individual rectangles and/or squares that connect orthogonally and are often 
perpendicular to each mass. 
b. Unbroken planes along a façade greater than 100 feet in length are not permitted. Create substantial 
facade articulation (minimum 4-foot separation between planes) instead of simply changing materials 
on the same plane/wall. Each elevation is subject to this requirement.  
c. Enclosed or covered walkways connecting two buildings is encouraged. 
d. Use additive elements, such as covered porches, entryways, to create a variety in the facade.  
e. Use of heavy timber columns and beams within the interior of the building to be seen from the 
exterior is strongly encouraged. 
5. Mixed-use buildings shall include retail and/or restaurant space on the ground level, oriented such that the 
primary entry faces the Green. Offices may be on the ground level, although these are encouraged on 
façades other than those facing the Green. Residential, such as Live-Work units, studio apartments, and/or 
offices are allowed above the ground level. 
a. Offices are encouraged on façades not adjacent to the Green. 
b. Businesses with ground floor façade frontage adjacent to the Green shall incorporate exterior space, 
such as seating or outdoor display areas, associated with the business. Alternatively, these exterior 
spaces may be shared across multiple businesses. 
c. The minimum exterior patio space per building shall be 15% of the building floor area (500 square feet 
minimum). 
d. The Owner reserves the right to impose additional controls on materials used for exterior paving, 
furniture, lighting, fences, lighting, and gates, to be approved as part of the tenant finish. 
6. Larger buildings should be broken into several smaller businesses or be designed to appear to be a 
collection of smaller stores. 
7. Individual restaurants are encouraged to have a gross leasable area of 2,000 square feet or less. 
G. Building Entries 
1. The primary entry (entries) to businesses along façades adjoining the Green shall be from the walkway 
along the green. 
2. Residential units and/or offices above the ground floor shall have a separate entry from ground floor 
businesses. 
3. Business entries include French doors, glass doors with transoms, glass doors with sidelites, double sliding 
doors, accordion doors, and decorative overhead garage doors. 
4. Secure service doors may be permitted, so long as they are visually unobtrusive and not directly adjacent 
to the Green. 
5. Service areas may be either between buildings or on façades not adjacent to the Green and screened to 
the extent reasonably possible from adjacent public right of way.  
H. Windows and Doors 
1. At least 50% of the ground floor façade of each building within 12’ of the finished floor elevation shall be 
transparent (windows, glass doors, and/or display windows), as measured from the outside edge of the 
frame. 
2. Upper levels shall include regular openings. Residences and offices are encouraged to employ decks, 
French doors, balconies, and similar to promote a human scale. 
I. 
Roofs 
1. Form: The basic roof form of each building shall be a gable, with a minimum slope of four feet vertical to 
twelve feet horizontal (4/12 slope). 

 
 
Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development 
Page C-3 
Standards & Guidelines (4th Amendment) 
Appendix C – Village Center Architectural Standards and Guidelines 
 
Dated: 2/14/2022 
2. Materials: standing seam metal, light to dark gray. 
3. Roof Slope: minimum of four feet vertical in twelve feet horizontal (4/12). 
a. Exceptions include rooftop decks and level areas rooftop utilities. 
b. Rooftop utilities shall be screened and integrated into the building design such that they are visible 
from neither the Green, nor adjoining public rights of way. 
4. Other roof design materials may include dormers, cupolas, hay hoods, and shed roofs above covered walkways. 
5. Roof drains: 
a. Rain gutters shall be a ‘U’ – shaped section, copper, aluminum, or black. 
b. Rain chains may be allowed to drain portions of roofs at or less than 500 square feet watershed. 
c. To the extent possible, roof drainage shall be directed to an underground storm drain system or 
otherwise away from sidewalks surrounding the building. 
J. 
Color Palette 
1. Each building shall have a body color, trim color and roof color recommended by the DRC prior to any Site 
Plan application to the City.  
2. Colors: 
a. Main body color: white, to a light gray color value. 
b. Accent trim: white, gray, or black. 
c. Natural wood may be used on exposed post & beam and doors. 
d. Doors may be other colors. 
K. Decks and Porches 
1. Juliet or full balconies are encouraged. These shall have a separate private entry that is secondary to any 
retail or office entry. 
2. Surfaces of visible deck materials must be a stained or colored composite decking material. 
3. Adjacent to Village green, colored concrete, may be allowed but must receive approval by the developer 
before installation. 
4. Roof top decks are encouraged. 
L. Building Materials 
1. Approved siding materials include fiber cement siding, synthetic or real stone, and board-form concrete. 
2. Fiber cement siding shall be in the pattern of lap siding, shiplap siding, panels, shingles, and/or board and 
batten. 
3. Synthetic or real stone must be monolithic in appearance without a large range in color values. 
M. Signs and Building Numbers: Proposed signs shall comply with the standards in PUD §3.B.3, subject to the 
following additional standards:  
1. Building numbers shall be black, or oil rubbed bronze. 
2. Signs on the façade facing the Village Green shall be less ≤20 square feet, as measured to the outside edge of copy. 
3. Allowable sign types include wall signs, window lettering and projections below the canopy. 
4. Business names and logos are prohibited from awnings. 
5. Flag mounted signs, pole signs, and/or monument signs, other than shared multi-tenant signs, will not be permitted. 
6. Shared multi-tenant signs advertising businesses will be proposed for City approval separately from this PUD. 
7. Nothing in this section is intended to prohibit temporary promotional materials, seasonal displays, etc., 
that may be erected from time-to-time advertising a business opening, festival, or similar.

N. Prohibited Materials: The following may not be used as building materials, but may be permitted in functional areas, 
if appropriately screened from the Village Green and the Greenway: Precast concrete; EIFS (Exterior Insulation & 
Finish System) cladding; Plywood and/or pressed board materials; Composition siding; Stacked logs; Plastic materials. 
O. Design Elements: In conjunction with the above, buildings in the Village Center shall include, at minimum, the 
number of items from each category based on the type of building proposed.

STAFF REPORT 
 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-63: Sweetgrass PUD Amendment  
REQUEST: 
An amendment to the Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD). 
LOCATION: 
Southeast corner of the intersection of College Drive and Avenue C.  
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning 
OWNER: 
Lummis Livestock CO, LLC 
CURRENT ZONING: 
PUD Ord. #4286 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential, Mixed-use Commercial, Open Space and Parks,  
and Mixed-use Employment 
TOTAL AREA: 
Approximately 2,349.5 acres 
CASE PLANNER: 
Connor White, Planner II

PUDC-23-63  
 
 
August 7, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the Sweetgrass PUD amendment with conditions as 
outlined in the staff report.   
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.2 Planned Development - Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan 
• 
UDC 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District 
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
• 
Current Sweetgrass PUD – Link (Original Ord. #4286, administratively amended as allowed by 
the PUD)  
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This is a request to amend the Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD). This is an amendment to 
reorganize the entire PUD, change the master plan for the area and make changes to the regulations.   
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the final Planned Unit Development 
(PUD), the Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.2.d – a detailed analysis of 
which is below. No conditions may be added to a final PUD.  The purpose of a final PUD review is to 
ensure compliance with the approved Preliminary PUD, its conditions of approval, and to establish the 
Ordinance officially changing the zoning to the PUD zone district.   
 
1) The application meets all threshold requirements for application of the PUD District or Form-
based Code standards. (UDC 2.2.2.d.1) 
 
When a new PUD is established the UDC requires the PUD to meet certain thresholds; however, 
this application is proposing amending the text of an existing PUD, not to establish or expand 
the PUD.  
 
The Unified Development Code requires every PUD Ordinance to include 12 specific elements 
(outlined in UDC 5.6.2.f). These elements are: 
1. 
Uses by right and location 
2. 
Uses permitted by Board approval and location 
3. 
Lot and building standards (including frontage, size, coverage, building height, and 
setbacks/build-to lines) 
4. 
Building design standards 
5. 
Landscape, buffering, and screening requirements 
6. 
Common open space or facilities and design types 
7. 
Signage requirements and regulations 
8. 
Parking requirements 
9. 
Development performance standards 
10. 
Proposed ownership of common facilities  

PUDC-23-63  
 
 
August 7, 2023 
11. 
A statement on how proposed deviations from otherwise required standards support 
the proposed plan, and result in design excellence and outstanding public amenity that 
is otherwise not allowable under general zoning standards 
12. 
Any other requirements and restrictions 
 
The existing PUD addresses all of the above items and the amendments continue to address the 
requirements. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application presents clear and specific criteria on how the property and subsequent 
development will be reviewed, regulated and modified consistent with these regulations. 
(UDC 2.2.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed substantive amendment to the PUD still has some ambiguity and some technical 
corrections and clarifications that will be needed. Staff will work with the applicant to fix the 
necessary technical and clarifications needed. The reorganization of the PUD does make it 
clearer to read.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
  
3) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.2.2.d.3) 
 
PlanCheyenne has several designations for this area. The future land use map designates a 
majority of the development as Urban Residential, but also has designations of Mixed-use 
Commercial, Open Space and Parks, and Mixed-use Employment.  
 
The Urban Residential category is intended to develop with largely residential uses with some 
neighborhood-serving commercial uses in developing areas. The proposed amendments address 
the market demand and better define the areas to be developed as residential. There are also 
text amendments that help to address workforce housing.  
 
Open Space and Parks is intended for regional parks and open space arreas. The proposed 
amendments do not effect the area designated as the park for this development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
4) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.2.2.d.4) 
 
The PUD amendment does propose changing the major street layout of the subdivision. Through 
staffs review it was found that the new proposed major street layout works better for the future 
build out of the subdivision. Open space is still being provided throughout Sweetgrass and block 
sizes will be reviewed with the individual plats that are submitted for the various pods. The 
proposed amendments are taking into consideration how the first few pods have developed and 
how the rest of the subdivision can develop as it expands.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

PUDC-23-63  
 
 
August 7, 2023 
5) The application meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.2.2.d.5) 
 
The amend does meet the purpose, intent and all applicable standards in Article 4. The master 
plan proposes a new street network that provides better movement through the subdivision as 
future phases are built.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
6) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.2.2.d.6) 
 
Phasing is proposed with this PUD. Sweetgrass is intended to be built out over thirty (30) 
phases. There is a master plan that demonstrates the intended layout of the development and 
indicates the zoning, uses and street networks.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.2.2.d.7) 
 
A traffic impact study was prepared at the time of the original PUD. A new study was prepared 
for this proposed amendment. The study addressed new major street layouts and potential uses 
and developments within the Sweetgrass subdivision.   
  
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
8) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas. (UDC 2.2.2.d.8) 
 
The proposed amendment is specific to the PUD and the area within the PUD. This amendment 
does not directly affect nearby properties. Staff has identified a concern with access (utility and 
vehicular) to lands to the southeast of this PUD. There appears to be a note on the map which 
allows for the extension of streets into this area.      
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
9) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared with the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of proposed zoning district. (UDC 2.2.2.d.9) 
 
This is a PUD amendment that changes some of the uses within the already established PUD. 
The changes made allow for additional uses in some areas of the subdivision and allow for more 
types of development.  
 

PUDC-23-63  
 
 
August 7, 2023 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
10) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.2.d.10) 
 
The proposed amendment does not change the intent or applicability of the PUD. The proposed 
amendment does not eliminate any amenities that are already required and does not affect the 
existing developments that have been built.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
11) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not 
executed after approval. (UDC 2.2.2.d.11) 
 
The changes made to the PUD can be met by future development within the Sweetgrass 
subdivision.  
 
Review criterion largely not applicable. 
   
12) Whether the planning and urban design concepts, and any proposed deviation from or 
alternative to otherwise applicable development standards, result in clear public benefits, as 
opposed to benefits to existing or future land owners in the development. (UDC 2.2.2.d.12) 
 
The proposed amendment to the PUD reorganizes the layout of the PUD, change the major 
street network and change some of the uses and locations the uses are allowed. The PUD also 
adds workforce housing as a use. The overall changes to the PUD benefit the public and make 
improvements to the larger subdivision.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
13) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.2.d.13) 
 
Staff has reviewed the proposed amendment and has found some aspects that need 
clarification, such as certain zones listed in the PUD, some of the uses and where they are or 
aren’t permitted, lighting regulations, side-loaded garages, exemptions, and landscaping. Staff is 
not opposed to the changes proposed but would like to clarify with the applicant that the 
proposed changes are being interpreted the way the applicant is intending.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
  
14) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.2.d.14) 
 
Generally, the amendment does not appear to harm the public health, safety, or welfare. An 
amenity is still being provided and design requirements are still present.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 

PUDC-23-63  
 
 
August 7, 2023 
15) The application is compatible with the character of the surrounding area in terms of building 
scale, building form, landscape, and site design. (UDC 2.2.2.d.15) 
 
The proposed amendment reorganizes the PUD, changes the street network for the 
development and makes changes to some of the regulations. The amendments proposed are 
within character with the surrounding area, as much of the area is part of this development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.2.(d) for Final 
Planned Unit Development (PUD) approval with conditions. 
  
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the PUD Ordinance. 
2. Recommend that the PUD Ordinance be approved with specific conditions or modifications, to 
be resolved prior to Governing Body approval of the PUD Ordinance.  
3. Recommend that the application be denied based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Options 
 
A PUD is a specialized zoning Ordinance that becomes the official zoning of the selected area of the 
community. Once published, the Ordinance becomes law. Conditions or modifications must be resolved 
prior to final Governing Body approval. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends that the Planning Commission recommend the Governing Body approve the 
Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD) amendment with conditions: 
1. The applicant will work with Staff to make technical corrections and clarifications to the PUD 
and PUD Maps.  
2. The applicant will work with Staff to make the necessary changes to clarify the aspects 
mentioned in criteria 13.   
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Master Application 
4. Proposed PUD Amendment

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_54823629.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING CHAPTER 8.68, LIMITING THE USE OF 
PLASTIC SINGLE USE CARRYOUT BAGS, OF TITLE 8, HEALTH AND 
SAFETY, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, REGULATING THE USE OF SINGLE USE PLASTIC BAGS 
AS SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Chapter 8.68, Limiting the Use of Single Use Plastic Bags, of Title 8, 
Health and Safety, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created as set out in this 
ordinance. 
 
Chapter 8.68 - LIMITING THE USE OF SINGLE USE PLASTIC BAGS 
 
8.68.010 – Purpose. 
 
The City of Cheyenne has the responsibility to protect the economy, natural environment and 
health of its citizens and guests and single use plastic bags create waste, pollute the city’s 
waterways, roadways, landscapes and harm wildlife populations. Accordingly, the purpose of this 
chapter is to abate the nuisance caused by the proliferation of single use plastic bags by eliminating 
the use of these bags by all retail establishments within the jurisdiction of the City of Cheyenne. 
 
8.68.020 – Definitions; applicability. 
 
A. As used in this chapter: 
 
1. "City" means the city of Cheyenne as defined by section 1.04.020 of the city code;  
 
2. "Department" means the compliance department for the city of Cheyenne; 
 
3. "Paper Grocery bag means a paper bag that contains a minimum of forty percent 
(40%) post consumer recycled content and is one hundred percent (100%) 
recyclable; 
 
4. "Retail establishment" means any retail business in a commercial setting that sells 
consumer goods of any kind directly to a customer, including and without 
limitation, clothing, household goods or personal items; 
 
5. "Reusable bag" means a bag with stitched handles that is specifically designed and 
manufactured for multiple reuse and is either: 
 
a. Made of cloth or other machine washable fabric; or 
 
11:58 am, Aug 09 2023

Page 2 of 4 
 
b. Other durable material suitable for reuse with stitched handles. 
 
6. "Single use plastic bag" means any bag made of plastic that is provided by a retail 
establishment to a customer at the point of sale or checkout, that is neither intended 
nor suitable or durable for continuous reuse as a carryout bag. 
 
B. This chapter shall not apply to the following: 
 
1. Laundry dry cleaning bags, door-hanger bags, newspaper bags or packages of 
multiple bags intended for use as garbage, pet waste or yard waste receptacles; 
 
2. Bags provided by pharmacists or veterinarians to contain prescription drugs or other 
medical necessities; 
 
3. Bags provided by a restaurant or other food establishment that sells food to be eaten 
on or off its business premises; 
 
 
4. Bags used by a non-profit corporation or other hunger relief charity to distribute 
food, grocery products, clothing or other household items; or 
 
5. Bags used by a consumer inside a retail establishment to: 
 
a. Contain bulk items, including but not limited to, produce, nuts, grains, candy or 
small hardware; 
 
b. Contain or wrap frozen foods, meat or fish; 
 
c. Contain or wrap flowers, potted plants or other items in order to prevent 
moisture damage to other purchases; or 
 
d. Contain unwrapped prepared foods or bakery goods. 
 
8.68.030 - Single use plastic bags prohibited; environmental service fee; penalty. 
 
A. No retail establishment shall provide or distribute a single use plastic bag to any 
customer. Retail establishments may only provide reusable bags, paper grocery bags or 
cardboard boxes at checkout to a customer. To further promote the use of reusable bags 
and reduce the quantity of single use carryout bags entering the city's waste stream, 
retail establishments are encouraged to make reusable bags free or for sale at checkout 
counters and to provide boxes for use at checkout counters free of charge. 
 
B. For any paper grocery bag provided to a customer by a retail establishment, the 
establishment shall charge the customer an environmental service fee of ten cents ($.10) 
per bag. Retail establishments shall specify on the customer transaction receipt the 
number of paper grocery bags provided and the total amount of the environmental 
service fees charged. It shall be a violation of this section for any retail establishment 

Page 3 of 4 
 
to pay or otherwise reimburse a customer for any portion of the environmental service 
fee. 
 
C. Fifty percent (50%) of the environmental service fee collected in accordance with this 
section shall be retained by the retail establishment for administrative costs incurred 
for collection and reporting of the service fee to the city as required under subsection 
(B) of this section. The remaining fifty percent (50%) of the environmental service fee 
shall be remitted on a quarterly basis to the city for use by the department in 
implementing and enforcing the requirements of this chapter and for the purposes of 
promoting this chapter and for public education regarding sustainability. Each quarterly 
payment shall be made not later than thirty (30) days after the end of the calendar 
quarter. 
 
D. Notwithstanding the requirements imposed under subsection (A) of this section, retail 
establishments shall not collect the environmental service fee from any person with a 
voucher or electronic benefits card issued under the Women, Infants and Children 
(WIC) Program, Emergency Food Assistance Program (TEFAP), Temporary 
Assistance to Needy Families Program (TANF) or the federal Supplemental Nutrition 
Assistance Program (SNAP). 
 
E. Nothing in this section shall be construed to prohibit retail establishments from making 
reusable bags available for sale to customers. 
 
8.68.040 - Enforcement, penalties and administrative remedies. 
 
A. The compliance department is assigned administrative responsibility of this chapter. 
The department shall be authorized to take any and all other actions reasonably 
necessary to enforce this chapter, including but not limited to investigating alleged 
violations of this chapter, including entering the premises of any retail establishment 
during business hours. 
 
B. If the director of the department determines that a violation of this chapter has occurred, 
the department shall issue to the retail establishment a written warning advising the 
establishment of the violation and that any subsequent and substantially similar 
violation may subject the retail establishment to the criminal penalties specified under 
subsection (C) of this section. 
 
C. A retail establishment that violates any of the requirements imposed under this chapter, 
after having received a written warning issued in accordance with subsection (B) of 
this section, is guilty of a misdemeanor and punishable by fine of one hundred dollars 
($100.00), for which the operator of the retail establishment shall be liable. Each day 
of a continuing violation shall constitute a distinct and separate offense. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 

Page 4 of 4 
 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication but no sooner than January 1, 2024.

THIRD AND FINAL READING: ____________________________________

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR 
 
 
(SEAL)

Background:

housing construction and safety standards.”

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 5.8.2.h OF THE UNIFIED 
DEVELOPMENT CODE (UDC), AMENDING MANUFACTURED HOMES 
ON SINGLE PLATTED PROPERTIES OR TRACTS." 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
Section 2.  That on August 21, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
Section 3.  That Section 5.8.2.h, Manufactured Homes on Single Platted Properties or 
Tracts, of Article 5, Zoning Regulations, Supplemental Provisions, of the Unified Development 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this section. 
1. The installation of all manufactured homes, appurtenances, foundations, and retaining
walls shall comply with all City adopted building codes and City Code. No manufactured
home will be permitted under this section if the date of manufacture is greater than eight
(8) years.
2 Manufactured homes shall have the delivery system removed, including the towing hitch, 
and shall be installed on a permanent foundation constructed of masonry, concrete or other 
approved material.  
3. Manufactured homes shall be built to the current HUD code as referenced in the federal
manufactured housing construction and safety standards.
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

WYOMING:

that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
Section 2.  That on August 21, 2023, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
Section 3.  That Section 5.8.2.h, Manufactured Homes on Single Platted Properties or 
Tracts, of Article 5, Zoning Regulations, Supplemental Provisions, of the Unified Development 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this section. 
1. The home shall be sited in such a fashion that it meets all the requirements of the district
in which it is located. The installation of all manufactured homes, appurtenances,
foundations, and retaining walls shall comply with all City adopted building codes and City
Code. No manufactured home will be permitted under this section if the date of
manufacture is greater than eight (8) years.
2 The homeManufactured homes shall have the delivery system removed, including the 
towing hitch, and shall be installed on a permanent foundation constructed of masonry, 
concrete or other approved material. The home shall be installed on a permanent foundation 
constructed of masonry, concrete or other approved material and shall comply with the 
building code. The foundation system shall be designed and constructed to sustain, within 
the stress limitations specified in the building code, all loads specified in the building code. 
The installation instructions as provided by the manufacturer of the manufactured home 
shall be used to determine permissible points of support for vertical loads and points of 
attachment for anchorage systems used to resist horizontal and uplift forces. The 
foundation system design shall be stamped and signed by a Wyoming licensed professional 
engineer. 
2. 3.  
The house Manufactured homes shall be built to the current HUD code for this 
zone as referenced in the federal manufactured housing construction and safety standards. 
Skirting and permanent perimeter enclosures shall be installed. Skirting shall be of 
material suitable for exterior exposure and contact with the ground. Permanent perimeter 
enclosures shall be constructed of materials as required by the building code for regular 
foundation construction. Skirting shall be installed in accordance with the skirting 
manufacturer’s installation instructions. Skirting shall be adequately secured to assure 
stability, to minimize vibration and susceptibility to wind damage, and to compensate for 
possible frost heave. 
11:35 am, Aug 22 2023

Page 2 of 3 
 
4. Where retaining walls are used as a permanent perimeter enclosure, they shall resist the 
lateral displacements of soil or other materials and shall conform to the building codes as 
specified for foundation walls. Retaining walls and foundation walls shall be constructed 
of approved treated wood, concrete, masonry or other approved materials or combination 
of materials as for foundations as specified in the building code. Siding materials shall 
extend below the top of the exterior of the retaining or foundation wall or the joint between 
siding and enclosure wall shall be flashed in accordance with the building code. 
5. The structure shall have a pitched roof, with a slope of not less than a nominal three inch 
vertical rise for each twelve (12) inches of horizontal run. Roof material shall consist of 
nonreflective material customarily used for conventional dwellings including but not 
limited to fiberglass shingles, composition shingles or tile material. Roof materials shall 
not include flat or corrugated sheet metal, except for manufactured metal roof panels. 
6. The structure shall have a roof overhang. of not less than eight inches measured from the 
vertical side of the home. 
7. The structure shall have siding material consisting of wood or wood products, stucco, brick, 
rock, horizontal lap steel or aluminum or horizontal lap vinyl. 
8. The structure shall be a minimum of 24’ along the widest part of the structure’s shortest 
axis.  
9. The structure shall be constructed in accordance with the most recent version of the HUD 
code. However, no home will be permitted under this section if it was built prior to the year 
2000. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PLANNING COMMISSION  
AUGUST 21, 2023 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 5.8.2.h. 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-23-1 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Sections 5.8.2.h as outlined in Option B of this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests 
meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendment came about because a citizen had submitted for a variance to UDC Section 
5.8.2.h.8, which required that any manufactured home installed on a single platted lot must be 24-feet 
wide. Through Staffs review we determined that the variance could not be supported because of the 
strictness of a variance. The proposed lot on which the applicant wanted to install a smaller manufactured 
home could easily hold a larger manufactured home that met the requirements. The proposed home by 
the applicant was a newer model and would be placed on a permanent foundation and meet all other 
requirements of this section, except for the width.  
 
Staff reviewed this section and discussed many of the requirements with the Chief Building Official. A 
stick-built home, or a home built to the International Residential Building Code (IRC) could be built to the 
size of the proposed manufactured home and not have to meet any of the requirements in section 5.8.2.h. 
Additionally, homes built to the IRC do not have design requirements per the UDC and can come in any 
shape or size. This means that someone could build a small home that looked like a manufactured home 
anywhere in the community.

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-23-1 
 
 
August 21, 2023 
 
Staff is proposing two amendment options. Option A would allow manufactured homes of any size to be 
installed on a single platted lot as long as they met all of the other requirements of section 5.8.2.h. Staff 
believes the most notable requirements are that a manufactured home would need to be installed on a 
permanent foundation, the delivery system would need to be completely removed and the manufactured 
home can not be more than 8 years old. These requirements create permanence. Option A also retains 
some of the design requirements under today’s code.  
 
Option B proposes only having four requirements. Those requirements are, the manufactured home will 
need to meet all City building code and zoning code requirements, be no more than eight years old, the 
delivery system needs to be removed, and the home must be placed on a permanent foundation. Much 
of this section of code is covered by building code regulations and can be removed from the zoning code. 
Homes built under the IRC can be smaller, the same shape, and have the same look as a manufactured 
home.   
 
The proposed modifications to this section would allow for more obtainable housing and move in the 
direction the Affordable Housing Task Force has been recommending. There is almost no difference if 
someone built a small home to IRC standards verses a small HUD built manufactured home. HUD 
requirements are getting closer to IRC requirements and the differences between them is become less 
and less.  
 
Staff prefers Option B as it would be easier to regulate, has less overlap with existing building code, and 
is similar to how any other residence is regulated. Option A is a good alternative that keeps in a lot of the 
existing language, and it would still be beneficial to the community.    
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Option A: 
Amend UDC Section 5.8.2.h, Manufactured Homes on Single Platted Properties or Tracts 
 
1. The home shall be sited in such a fashion that it meets all the requirements of the district in which 
it is located. The installation of all manufactured homes, appurtenances, foundations, and 
retaining walls shall comply with all City adopted building codes and City standards applicable at 
the time of permit submittal. 
2. The home Manufactured homes shall have the delivery system removed, including the towing 
hitch. 
3. The home Manufactured homes shall be installed on a permanent foundation constructed of 
masonry, concrete or other approved material. and shall comply with the building code. The 
foundation system shall be designed and constructed to sustain, within the stress limitations 
specified in the building code, all loads specified in the building code. The installation instructions 
as provided by the manufacturer of the manufactured home shall be used to determine 
permissible points of support for vertical loads and points of attachment for anchorage systems 
used to resist horizontal and uplift forces. The foundation system design shall be stamped and 
signed by a Wyoming licensed professional engineer. 
4. 3. Manufactured homes shall be built to Manufactured Home Construction and Safety Standards 
(HUD Code) the current HUD code for this zone as referenced in the federal manufactured 
housing construction and safety standards. Skirting and permanent perimeter enclosures shall 
be installed. Skirting shall be of material suitable for exterior exposure and contact with the 
ground. Permanent perimeter enclosures shall be constructed of materials as required by the 
building code for regular foundation construction. Skirting shall be installed in accordance with

REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The proposed amendment is in compliance with multiple goals and policies such as: Policy 1.1.B 
Flexibility to respond to market demands, 1.4.A Infill Opportunities, 2.5.A Community Housing 
needs met, and 2.5.B Workforce Housing throughout the community, to name a few.    
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and 
other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 

PLTA-23-1 
 
 
August 21, 2023 
 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
(E)  Location and use of buildings, structures and land for 
trade, industry, residence or other purposes. 
 (ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to change the 
requirements of manufactured homes when installed on a single platted lot.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would allow for smaller manufactured homes to be installed on single platted 
lots throughout the community and provided additional avenues for affordable or obtainable 
housing in the community.  
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that both options of this text amendment meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Sections 5.8.2.h as 
outlined in Option B of this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meets the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.
